There isn't really anything else I look forward to, so... no, just what I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you have a low or sad mood most of the time, even if you're not necessarily crying or feeling overwhelmed by sadness?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was just saying, I wouldn't describe it as sad or low. It's... well, it's just that empty feeling. I don't walk around feeling sad, I just feel like there's nothing there at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep or appetite lately, or do you find yourself feeling worthless or guilty about things?\n\nPatient: No, my sleep and appetite are about the same as they've always been. I sleep fine, and I eat my regular meals. And... no, I don't feel worthless or guilty about anything. I just feel kind of blank, like I told you.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if this lack of pleasure or interest in things has been continuous or if it comes and goes over time?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't exactly kept track of it, to be honest. It feels like it's just... well, mostly been there lately. I haven't really noticed it coming and going or changing much from day to day. It's just that same blank feeling I mentioned. So I couldn't say for certain.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts have been slower than usual, or that you're moving more slowly than you used to, like everything feels kind of slowed down?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't feel slowed down at all.\n\nDoctor: How long has this feeling of emptiness and lack of enjoyment been going on?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about two weeks now. It's like... well, it's like the part of me that could be glad about those things, or even just comforted by them, has just shut off. Nothing seems to bring that feeling back right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period of time before this one where you felt this same kind of emptiness or lack of pleasure for a long time, or is this the first time you've felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, I... So I couldn't say for certain.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts have been slower than usual, or that you're moving more slowly than you used to, like everything feels kind of slowed down?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. My thinking seems fine, and I'm moving around just the same as I always do. I don't feel slowed down at all.\n\nDoctor: How long has this feeling of emptiness and lack of enjoyment been going on?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about two weeks now. It started right after... well, right after my husband collapsed. The paramedics were working on him right there in our kitchen, and ever since then, I've just felt this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level, concentration, or the way you see yourself, such as feeling more tired than usual, having trouble focusing, or thinking less of yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, focusing has definitely been hard. I used to plan all our meals and keep my calendar straight without any trouble. But now... I just stare at a grocery list or the senior-center schedule, and my mind just goes blank. So yes, the trouble focusing is very real right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you completely lost interest or pleasure in all activities, not just the garden or your family, so that nothing gives you joy anymore?\n\nPatient: Yes... yes, I have. Even when my children come over and sit with me, or when the sun is shining right out on my garden, I just feel completely flat inside. It's like... well, it's like the part of me that could be glad about those things, or even just comforted by them, has just shut off. Nothing seems to bring that feeling back right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period of time before this one where you felt this same kind of emptiness or lack of pleasure for a long time, or is this the first time you've felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, I... I really don't think so. This is the first time. I've been sad before, of course, when my parents passed, or just through the ups and downs of life. But this... this complete emptiness, where I just can't feel anything at all... no, I've never experienced anything like this before. It's completely new to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in the past where your mood was low or you lost interest in things for a period of two weeks or more, to the point where it affected your daily life?\n\nPatient: No, I don't believe so. Like I said, I've had sad days in the past, but never a time where it lasted for weeks and stopped me from living my life. This feeling right now is the first time it's ever been like this.\n\nDoctor: Since your husband collapsed, have you experienced any days where you felt intense sadness, hopelessness, or thoughts of ending your life, even briefly?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any thoughts of ending my life. I've been terribly sad since it happened, and just... empty, like we talked about. But I haven't thought about hurting myself or anything like that. It's just a very deep, heavy sadness that doesn't seem to lift.\n\nDoctor: Has there been any moment during these past two weeks when something happened that you would normally find pleasurable or emotionally moving, and if so, did you feel even a little bit better or more emotionally engaged at that time?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. It's like the part of me that could be glad about things, or even just comforted, just... shut off.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don't enjoy activities you used to like, such as hobbies, spending time with family, or anything else that used to bring you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yes, well, that's what I meant just now about my children and the garden. It's not like I'm sad or sitting around crying all day. Like I was saying, it's more of an empty feeling... I just don't feel much of anything at all, rather than feeling low.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other things you used to enjoy or look forward to that you now feel indifferent about, or has this loss of enjoyment been limited to the things you already mentioned?\n\nPatient: No, I really can't think of anything else. Like I said, I just don't do much else nowadays, so it's really just the garden and my family where I notice it. There isn't really anything else I look forward to, so... no, just what I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you have a low or sad mood most of the time, even if you're not necessarily crying or feeling overwhelmed by sadness?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was just saying, I wouldn't describe it as sad or low. It's... well, it's just that empty feeling. I don't walk around feeling sad, I just feel like there's nothing there at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep or appetite lately, or do you find yourself feeling worthless or guilty about things?\n\nPatient: No, my sleep and appetite are about the same as they've always been. I sleep fine, and I eat my regular meals. And... no, I don't feel worthless or guilty about anything. I just feel kind of blank, like I told you.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if this lack of pleasure or interest in things has been continuous or if it comes and goes over time?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't exactly kept track of it, to be honest. It feels like it's just... well, mostly been there lately. I haven't really noticed it coming and going or changing much from day to day. It's just that same blank feeling I mentioned.
